\eta\mapsto\rho(\eta_{ij}),

where
\rho
is any seminorm of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{(\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}(\Lambda)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
.

Proof.

1.

We have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{1*}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}\psi_{i}^{*}(\psi_{i}^{-1*}A^{1}_{ij}\eta)A^{1*}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(A^{1}_{ij}\eta)A^{1*}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I}\chi_{i}\eta

\displaystyle=\eta.

Note that the sum is locally finite and thus each seminorm vanishes on all but finitely many summands, so it is a convergent sum in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
.

2.

For any chart
\psi
and bundle coordinate
A
, we have

\psi^{-1*}(A\eta)=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}((A\chi_{i}A^{1*}_{ij})\circ\psi^{-1})\psi^{-1*}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij}).

wavefront calculus on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
gives us

\WF(\psi^{-1*}(A\eta))\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}(\psi_{i}\circ\psi^{-1})^{*}\WF(\eta_{ij}).

Thus we have

\WF(\eta)=\bigcup\limits_{A,\psi}\psi^{*}\WF(\psi^{-1*}(A\eta))\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}(\psi_{i})^{*}\WF(\eta_{ij}).

The reverse inclusion follows from the fact that all
\psi_{i}
and
A^{1}_{ij}
are allowed choices for
A
and
\psi
.

3.

For
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(X)
, let
I_{\chi}
denote the (finite) set of
i\in I
such that
\supp(\chi)\cap U_{i}
is nonempty. For a bundle coordinate
A
, we have (on
U_{i}
)

A=\sum\limits_{j\leq\rk(E_{1})}A(A^{1*}_{ij})A^{1}_{ij}.

Thus for any
\eta\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
and any
k,V,\chi,\psi,A
such that the following is a viable seminorm, we have

\displaystyle\|\eta\|_{k,V,\chi,\psi,A}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\xi\in V}(|\xi|+1)^{k}|\mathcal{F}((\psi^{-1})^{*}(\chi A\eta))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\leq\rk(E_{1}),i\in I_{\chi}}\sup\limits_{\xi\in V}(|\xi|+1)^{k}|\mathcal{F}((\psi^{-1})^{*}(\chi A(\chi_{i}\psi_{i}^{*}(\eta_{ij})A^{1*}_{ij})(\xi)|

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j\leq\rk(E_{1}),i\in I_{\chi}}\|(\psi_{i}\circ\psi^{-1})^{*}(((A(\chi_{i}A^{1*}_{ij}))\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})\eta_{ij})\|_{k,V,\chi\circ\psi}.

As pushforwards by diffeomorphisms and multiplication with
C_{c}^{\infty}
-functions are continuous on wavefront spaces in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we obtain that any wavefront seminorm of
\eta
is bounded by the wavefront seminorms of the
\eta_{ij}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{(\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}(\Lambda)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
. Conversely for any of the latter seminorms, we have

\|\eta_{ij}\|_{k,V,\chi}=\|\eta\|_{k,V,\chi\circ\psi_{i}^{-1},\psi_{i},A_{ij}}.

Concerning the non-wavefront seminorms, i.e. those of the strong topology on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, we know that pull-back by a diffeomorphism and application of a bundle coordinate are continuous, so the
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
-seminorms of
\eta_{ij}
are bounded in terms of those of
\eta
. Conversely, by the formula in the first part of this lemma and continuity of multiplying with a smooth section and extending compactly supported distributions by 0,
the
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
-seminorms of
\eta
are bounded in terms of those of the
\eta_{ij}
.∎

We proceed to prove Theorem
2.7.11
:

Theorem

(Wavefront calculus on manifolds)
.

1.

The seminorms defined for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
are finite. In case
X
is a subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
E_{1}=X\times{\mathbb{C}}
, the new definition coincides with the earlier one.

2.

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
is complete.

3.

\Gamma_{c}(E_{1})
is dense in any
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
.

4.

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\emptyset}(E_{1})
coincides with
\Gamma(E_{1})
as a topological vector space.

5.

Differential operators between sections of
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
map
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{2})
continuously.

6.

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a smooth map, let
\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}\subseteq\dot{T}^{*}(X)
and
\Theta\subseteq\dot{T}^{*}(Y)
be closed conic subsets.

•

If
f^{*}(\Theta)
does not intersect the zero section,
f^{*}
extends to a continuous map from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Theta}(F)
to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{f^{*}(\Theta)}(f^{*}(F))
.

•

If
C\subseteq X
is a closed set such that
f|_{C}
is proper,
f_{*}
defines a continuous map from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(f^{*}F)|_{C}
to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{f_{*}(\Lambda)}(F)
.

•

If
\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime}
does not intersect the zero section, pointwise ”multiplication” of sections in
E_{1}\otimes E_{2}^{*}
and
E_{2}\otimes E_{3}
, defined at each point by

(a\otimes b)\cdot(c\otimes d):=b(c)\cdot(a\otimes d)

extends to a hypocontinuous bilinear map

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1}\otimes E_{2}^{*})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{2}\otimes E_{3})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{1}\otimes E_{3}).

•

The tensor product defines a hypocontinuous bilinear map

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})\times D^{\prime}_{\Theta}(F)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda\bar{\times}\Theta}(E_{1}\boxtimes F).

•

If
\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime}
does not intersect the zero section and
K\subseteq X
is compact, evaluation of distributions extends to hypocontinuous bilinear maps

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{1}^{*})|_{K}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}

and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{1}^{*})|_{K}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}.

These are symmetric, in the sense that

\zeta[\eta]=\eta[\zeta].

Proof.

1.

Let
\|\cdot\|_{k,V,\chi,\psi,A}
be a seminorm of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
and let
\eta\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
. Then
\supp(\chi\circ\psi^{-1})\times V
does not intersect
(\psi^{-1})^{*}(\Lambda)
and

\psi^{*}(\WF((\psi^{-1})^{*}(A\eta)))\subseteq\WF(\eta)\subseteq\Lambda.

Thus

\supp(\chi\circ\psi^{-1})\times V

does not intersect
\WF((\psi^{-1})^{*}(\chi A\eta))
, hence
\|\cdot\|_{k,V,\chi\circ\psi}
is a seminorm of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\WF((\psi^{-1})^{*}(A\eta))}
, whence

\|\eta\|_{k,V,\chi,\psi,A}=\|(\psi^{-1})^{*}(A\eta)\|_{k,V,\chi\circ\psi}

is finite.

In case
X\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
E_{1}=X\times{\mathbb{C}}
, choosing
\psi
to be the identity and
A
the map that forgets the basepoint, we obtain the original wavefront norms. As this is a cover with a set of local frames, Lemma
6.2.7
implies that the two sets of seminorms are equivalent. This proves the first claim.

2.

Suppose
(\eta_{s})_{s\in S}
is a Cauchy net in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
. Then in particular it is also Cauchy in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(E_{1})
, which has less seminorms, so it converges to a limit
\eta
there. This implies that all components
(\eta_{s})_{ij}
converge to the components
\eta_{ij}
of the limit distributionally. All components
(\eta_{s})_{ij}
are also Cauchy nets in their corresponding wavefront spaces. As the wavefront spaces in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are complete, these components converge. As the wavefront spaces are continuously embedded in the spaces of all distributions, the limits in the wavefront space must also be
\eta_{ij}
. As the seminorms of the
\eta_{ij}-(\eta_{s})_{ij}
are equivalent to those of
\eta-\eta_{s}
, this implies that
\eta_{s}
converges to
\eta
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
.

3.

For each
i
and
j
, let
(\eta_{ijn})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence of smooth functions converging to
\eta_{ij}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{(\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}(\Lambda)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
. Set

\eta_{n}:=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ijn})A^{1*}_{ij}.

For any viable wavefront seminorm, we have

\displaystyle\|\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ijn})A^{1*}_{ij}-\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{1*}_{ij}\|_{k,V,\chi,\psi,A}

\displaystyle=\|(((AA^{1*}_{ij})\chi_{i})\circ\psi)(\psi_{i}\circ\psi^{-1})^{*}(\eta_{ijn}-\eta_{ij})\|_{k,V,\chi\circ\psi^{-1}}.

As multiplication and pullback are continuous in wavefront norms on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, this converges to zero. As all operations involved are distributionally continuous, we also have convergence in all seminorms of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(E_{1})
. We thus have as a limit in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Gamma}(E_{1})
:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\eta_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ijn})A^{1*}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{1*}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\eta.

As
\eta
was arbitrary and all
\eta_{n}
are smooth, this concludes the proof.

4.

As
\eta
is smooth if and only if all
\eta_{ij}
are smooth and is in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\emptyset}(E_{1})
is and only if all
\eta_{ij}
are in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\emptyset}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
, equality as sets follows from the corresponding statement on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Moreover, we also know that the
C^{\infty}
- and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\emptyset}
-norms of
\eta_{ij}
are equivalent. We have already seen that the
D^{\prime}_{\emptyset}
-seminorms of the
\eta_{ij}
are equivalent to those of
\eta
. To conclude the proof, we thus have to show that the
C^{\infty}
-seminorms of
\eta
and of the
\eta_{ij}
are equivalent. We once again use the equalities

\eta_{ij}=\psi_{i}^{-1*}(A^{1}_{ij}\eta)

and

\eta=\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{1*}_{ij}.

As all opertations involved are continuous with respect to
C^{\infty}
-seminorms, we can conclude that the
C^{\infty}
-seminorms of
\eta
are equivalent to those of the
\eta_{ij}
. Putting everything together, we obtain that the
D^{\prime}_{\emptyset}
- and
C^{\infty}
-norms of
\eta
are equivalent.

5.

Differential operators also act as differential operators in coordinates, i.e. for every differential operator
D
there are differential operators
D_{ijk}
such that
(D\eta)_{ij}=\sum\limits_{k\leq\rk(E_{1})}D_{ijk}\eta_{ik}
. As differential operators are continuous with respect to wavefront seminorms on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
D
is continuous with respect to the seminorms of the component and hence with respect to the seminorms of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{2})
.

6.

The idea of proof is always to consider the components
\eta_{ij}
and thus reduce the problem to wavefront calculus on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

•

pull-back:

Assume without loss of generality that for any
i\in I
, there is
l(i)\in I^{\prime}
such that
U_{i}\subseteq f^{-1}(U^{\prime}_{l(i)})
. This can always be arranged by refining the
U_{i}
. The
B_{l(i)j}
then pull back to a frame of
f^{*}(F)^{*}
over
U_{i}
, which we shall use as our frame to define
(f^{*}\eta)_{ij}
, i.e. we set for this part

A^{4}_{ij}:=f^{*}(B_{l(i),j}).

Suppose that
\eta\in\Gamma(F)
. If
f^{*}(\Theta)

does not intersect the zero section, then the same is true of

(\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}\circ f\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}({\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}}^{-1*}(\Theta))\subseteq\psi_{i}^{-1*}(f^{*}\Theta)

for any
i\in I
.
Thus
(\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}\circ f\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}
maps
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{{\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}}^{-1*}(\Theta)}(\Ran(\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}))

to

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(f^{*}\Theta)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
continuously. This means the corresponding seminorms of

(f^{*}\eta)_{ij}=(\psi_{l(i)}^{\prime}\circ f\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}(\eta_{ij})

are bounded in terms of those of
\eta_{ij}
. We can conclude that the seminorms of
f^{*}(\eta)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{f^{*}\Theta}(f^{*}(F))
are bounded in terms of the seminorms of
\eta
in
D^{\prime}_{\Theta}(F)
, so
f^{*}
extends to a continuous map between those two spaces.

•

push-forward

Choose the cover of
Y
in such a way that every
U_{i}^{\prime}
is precompact and consider an arbitrary
i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}
. Then
f^{-1}(U_{i})\cap C
is contained in a compact set and thus only intersects the support of finitely many
\chi_{i}
. Let
I_{i^{\prime}}\subseteq I
be the indices of these functions, i.e. the set of indices
i
such that

\supp(\chi_{i})\cap f^{-1}(U^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}})\cap C\neq\emptyset.

For any
\eta\in\Gamma(E_{1})
with support in
C
, we then have for
i\notin I_{i^{\prime}}
:

\supp(\chi_{i}\eta)\cap(f^{-1}(U^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}))=\emptyset.

For any chart
\psi
, let
\rho_{\psi}
be the smooth density function such that
\psi^{*}=\rho_{\psi}\psi^{-1}_{*}
(i.e. the volume density for the coordinate
\psi
). We then have

\psi^{-1*}=(\psi^{*})^{-1}=(\rho_{\psi}\psi^{-1}_{*})^{-1}=\psi_{*}\Big(\rho_{\psi}^{-1}\cdot\Big).

Using the above, we have for
j^{\prime}\leq\rk(F)
:

\displaystyle f_{*}(\eta)_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=f_{*}\Big(\sum\limits_{i\in I,j\leq\rk(E_{1})}\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{4*}_{ij}\Big)_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I_{i^{\prime}},j\leq\rk(F)}{\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}^{-1*}B_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}f_{*}(\chi_{i}(\psi_{i}^{*}\eta_{ij})A^{4*}_{ij})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I_{i^{\prime}},j\leq\rk(F)}\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}*}\rho_{\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}B_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}f_{*}(\chi_{i}(\rho_{\psi_{i}}^{-1}{\psi_{i*}^{-1}}\eta_{ij})A^{4*}_{ij})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in I_{i^{\prime}},j\leq\rk(F)}\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}*}f_{*}{\psi_{i*}^{-1}}((f^{*}(\rho_{\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}^{-1}B_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})\chi_{i}\rho_{\psi_{i}}A^{4*}_{ij})\circ\psi_{i}^{-1}\ \eta_{ij})

\displaystyle=:\sum\limits_{i\in I_{i^{\prime}},j\leq\rk(F)}(\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}\circ f\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})_{*}(\nu\eta_{ij}),

where
\nu
denotes the smooth compactly supported function
\eta_{ij}
is multiplied with in the line before. Multiplication with
\nu
is continuous in the appropriate wavefront space and ensures that the support condition for aplying the pushforward theorem on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
are met (every continuous function from a compact space to a Hausdorff space is proper). Thus all relevant seminorms of

(\psi^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}\circ f\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})^{*}(\nu\eta_{ij})

are bounded in terms of those of the
\eta_{ij}
and hence in terms of the seminorms of
\eta
. By the above, the same is then true for the seminorms of
f_{*}(\eta)_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
and thus for those of
f_{*}(\eta)
. This means that
f_{*}
extends to a continuous map from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(f^{*}(F))|_{C}
to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{f_{*}(\Lambda)}(F)
.

•

product:

On tensor product bundles, use the tensor products of the individual frames as the new reference frames, indexed by pairs of individual indices.
Fix a bounded subset
B\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1}\otimes E_{2}^{*})
. Then for any
i\in I
,
j_{1}\leq\rk(E_{1})
and
j_{2}\leq\rk(E_{2})
, the set

B_{i,(j_{1},j_{2})}:=\{\eta_{i,(j_{1},j_{2})}\mid\eta\in B\}

is bounded in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
, as the seminorms of the
\eta_{i(j_{1},j_{2})}
there are bounded in terms of the seminorms of
\eta
, which must be bounded on
B
.
Consider
\eta\in B
and
\zeta\in\Gamma(E_{2}\otimes E_{3})
and fix
i
,
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
as above. We have

\displaystyle(\eta\zeta)_{i(j_{1},j_{2})}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{i}^{-1*}((A^{1}_{ij_{1}}\otimes A^{3}_{ij_{2}})\eta\zeta)

\displaystyle=\psi_{i}^{-1*}\Big((A^{1}_{ij_{1}}\otimes A^{3}_{ij_{2}})\sum\limits_{k_{1}\leq\rk(E_{1})}\sum\limits_{l_{1}\leq\rk(E_{2})}\psi_{i}^{*}(\eta_{i(l_{1},k_{1})})A^{1*}_{il_{1}}\otimes A^{2}_{ik_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum\limits_{l_{2}\leq\rk(E_{2})}\sum\limits_{k_{2}\leq\rk(E_{3})}\psi_{i}^{*}(\zeta_{i(l_{2},k_{2})})A^{2*}_{il_{2}}\otimes A^{3*}_{ik_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{l_{1},k_{1},l_{2},k_{2}\text{ as above}}\eta_{i(l_{1},k_{1})}\zeta_{i(l_{2},k_{2})}\psi_{i}^{-1*}(A^{1}_{ij_{1}}(A^{1*}_{il_{1}})A^{2}_{ik_{1}}(A^{2*}_{il_{2}})A^{3}_{ij_{2}}(A^{3*}_{ik_{2}}))

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{l_{1},k_{1},l_{2},k_{2}\text{ as above}}\eta_{i(l_{1},k_{1})}\zeta_{i(l_{2},k_{2})}\delta_{j_{1}l_{1}}\delta_{k_{1}l_{2}}\delta_{j_{2}k_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\leq\rk(E_{2})}\eta_{i(j_{1},k)}\zeta_{i(k,j_{2})}

The product as a map

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda)}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda^{\prime})}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime})}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))

is hypocontinuous by wavefront calculus on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
Thus for every seminorm
\rho
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime})}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
, there are seminorms
(\rho^{\prime}_{kl})
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\psi_{i}^{-1*}(\Lambda^{\prime})}(\Ran(\psi_{i}))
and seminorms
(\rho^{\prime\prime}_{l})
of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{2}\otimes E_{3})
such that for some
N,N^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
C_{k},C\in{\mathbb{R}}
(depending on
B
, but not
\eta
), we have

\displaystyle\rho((\eta\zeta)_{i(j_{1},j_{2})})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{k\leq\rk(E_{2})}\rho(\eta_{i(j_{1},k)}\zeta_{i(k,j_{2})})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{k\leq\rk(E_{2})}C_{k}\sum\limits_{l=0}^{N}\rho^{\prime}_{kl}(\zeta_{i(k,j_{2})})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{l=0}^{N^{\prime}}C\rho^{\prime\prime}_{l}(\zeta).

As the seminorms of
\eta\zeta
in
D^{\prime}_{\Lambda\bar{+}\Lambda^{\prime}}
can be estimated by the component norms above,
\zeta\mapsto\eta\zeta
extends contiuosly to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{2}\otimes E_{3})
for any fixed
\eta
, and the extension satisfies the first half of the definition of hypocontinuity, i.e. the part with bounded sets in the first component.
As multiplication is (up to renaming and reordering vector bundles) symmetric in both arguments, we also obtain an extension of multiplication of sections that satisfies the second half of the definition of hypocontinuity. As both maps are continuous on products of bounded sets and convergent sequences are bounded, approximation by sequences of smooth sections shows that both extensions coincide. Thus we get a single extension that is hypocontinuous.

•

tensor product:
To avoid having to do a similar proof as for multiplication again, we reduce this to what we have already shown. Let
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
denote the projections of
X\times Y
on the first and second factor. Then for smooth sections
\eta
and
\zeta
of
E_{1}
and
F
, we have

\eta\otimes\zeta=\pi_{1}^{*}(\eta)\cdot\pi_{2}^{*}(\zeta).

The previous results yield that

\pi_{1}^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda\times 0}(\pi_{1}^{*}(E_{1}))

and

\pi_{2}^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Theta}(F)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0\times\Theta}(\pi_{2}^{*}(F))

are continuous and that multiplication is continuous as a map

\cdot\colon\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda\times 0}(\pi_{1}^{*}(E_{1}))\otimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0\times\Theta}(\pi_{2}^{*}(F))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda\bar{\times}\Theta}(E_{1}\boxtimes F),

as non-zero vectors in different components cannot add up to zero. Thus the composition

\cdot\circ(\pi_{1}^{*}\times\pi_{2}^{*})

yields a hypocontinuous extension of the tensor product.

•

evaluation
Fix
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(X)
that is 1 around
K
.
For
\phi\in\Gamma(E_{1}^{*})
supported in
K
and
\eta\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
, we have

\eta[\phi]=\eta[\phi\chi]=\phi\eta[\chi].

As multiplication is hypocontinuous on the appropriate spaces and evaluation is continuous, the map

(\eta,\zeta)\mapsto\eta[\zeta]:=\zeta\eta(\chi)

is a hypocontinuous extension of evaluation to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{1}^{*})|_{K}
.
In case both
\eta
and
\zeta
are smooth, we have

\zeta[\eta]=\eta[\zeta].

Thus, defining

\zeta[\eta]:=\eta[\zeta]

for more general distributions yields a hypocontinuous map on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}(E_{1}^{*})|_{K}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}(E_{1})
that agrees with evaluation in case both arguments are smooth. As smooth fuctions are dense in the appropriate spaces, it agrees with evaluation wherever evaluation is defined.∎
